[09:13:59.000] In the box
[09:14:00.000] So good evening, Chairwoman Lee and members of the committee on Budget and Government Operations and members of the City Council. Again, I'm Randy Connor, commissioner of the Chicago Department of Water Management, and I am pleased to present our 2027 Budget Proposal
[09:14:15.000] In my time leading DWM, I have seen the work of this department change in ways I could not have predicted. Our mission remains the same, providing safe, reliable drinking water, maintaining the infrastructure Chicagoans depend on every day. But the challenges facing our city have changed and the way we do our work must change with them
[09:14:32.000] Climate change, agent infrastructure, evolving water quality requirements, and the historic effort to replace lead service lines demand new approaches, strong partnerships and careful management of public resources.
[09:14:44.000] Our 2027 budget supports that work while ensuring we continue to deliver the essential services residents count on
[09:14:51.000] Water quality remains our highest priority. DWM distributes approximately 750 million gallons of drinking water daily to 5.1 million people through more than 4,300 miles of water main
[09:15:02.000] We conduct more than 600,000 water quality analysis annually, and continuously monitor our treatment system in Lake Michigan to identify and address potential concerns.
[09:15:13.000] Following years of research and consultation with the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency, we completed our transition to an increased dosage of orthophosphate for corrosion control in April 202
[09:15:24.000] Since that change, we have observed an average 40% decrease in lead concentrations throughout the city.
[09:15:32.000] This progress demonstrates why we must continually evaluate our practices and be prepared to improve them as science and standards evolve.
[09:15:40.000] We are also adapting to climate change, creating increasingly intense storms that place tremendous pressure on Chicago's century-old sewer system.
[09:15:48.000] When heavy rainfall overwhelms portions of our system, the consequences for residents can be devastating. DWM is expanding its toolbox to address flooding through targeted investments, innovative engineering and partnerships.
[09:16:00.000] In partnership with the MWRD, we are piloting two underground wing storage projects on the west side. Together, these facilities will provide nearly 1.7 million gallons of additional stormwater storage that are expected to provide flood protection for more than 2,600 homes
[09:16:16.000] Businesses and other properties. During heavy rainfall, the structures temporarily hold stormwater until the surrounding sewer system can accommodate its gradual release.
[09:16:26.000] We are also implementing our downspout disconnection program on the south side, directing clean rainwater toward lawns and green infrastructure, rather than the sewer system.
[09:16:35.000] Our citywide rain blocker and low flow catch basin lid programs slow the rate at which water into sewers during storms. While these measures may mean water remains on the streets longer, keeping that water out of overloaded sewers can help prevent basement backups.
[09:16:49.000] No single solution will eliminate flooding, but these efforts allow us to test and expand approaches that can make a meaningful difference for communities.
[09:16:59.000] Less surface line replacement represents another fundamental shift in our work with more than 400,000 lead service lines across Chicago, removing them is a massive multi-year, multi-billion dollar infrastructure undertaking.
[09:17:12.000] We have modified two of our lead line programs to accelerate replacement
[09:17:17.000] Now, under leaks and breaks program, when crews determined that alleged service line break is minor and his initial repair can be avoided, they can expedite homeowner outreach and move directly to a full lead line replacement.
[09:17:29.000] This approach eliminates the cost of an interim repair while advancing our replacement goals
[09:17:35.000] With the goal of replacing lead service lines at every licensed daycare in Chicago, our daycare program prioritizes those in lower income census, but will perform replacements for any qualified operator that request one.
[09:17:47.000] Committed to equity, we have worked with the Department of Procurement Services to divide larger contracts and opportunities that are more accessible to minority and women-owned businesses through the Small Business Initiative.
[09:17:59.000] With most of our programs replacing both public and private portions of eligible list service lines at no cost to property owners, sustained funding is essential.
[09:18:09.000] We will continue working with our federal and state partners to leverage available resources and move this work forward as efficiently and equitably as possible.
[09:18:17.000] Adapting to new challenges also means improving how we maintain our existing infrastructure. Last year I discussed pilot projects designed to improve our operational efficiency while delivering services cost effectively.
[09:18:30.000] Those pilots are now becoming established programs
[09:18:33.000] DWM's responsibility extends beyond maintaining today's infrastructure. We must prepare for tomorrow's challenges while managing the costs and competing demands facing a major urban water utility.
[09:18:45.000] Our 2027 budget advances public health, strengthens flood mitigation, improves operational efficiency, and modernizes the infrastructure Chicago will pass on to future generations, building on the dedication of our employees and strong partnerships with the City Council
[09:19:00.000] Community stakeholders and other levels of government by investing strategically and remaining vigilant, responsive, and willing to adapt, DW will continue to deliver the safe, reliable water and sewer services the Chicago deserve. Thank you.
